a deterministic `classSweepRetryFeedback` fires at the 2nd instance of a class (2 ledger findings or seenCount≥2) — "SWEEP THE CLASS: enumerate ALL sibling sites, fix every one, list the enumeration in reviewResponses" — injected by both convergence loops on review rejections (run 06-19's 4-round loop trips at round 2). **(F2)** design/spec writer prompts carry the derivation standing rule: paired generate/validate contracts must be DERIVED from the actual registry via a stated rule and shipped with the enumerated closure table — never hand-written on both sides. **(F3)** both reviewer prompts gain the Codex-calibrated finding quality bar: discrete/actionable/current-revision defects with provable impact (no speculation), one finding per defect, severity P0–P3 with fixed semantics (P0/P1 feed the existing HIGH_SEVERITY_RE scan), per-finding confidence, "zero findings is a valid, respected outcome", and an explicit gate-ownership list (id formats, bidirectional coverage, phases shape, dimension shape, deliverable checks, verdict vocabulary) so reviewers stop hand-simulating deterministic gates. **(F4)** implementer + fixer prompts carry worktree discipline: NEVER revert or delete changes you did not make; name blocking foreign changes instead of fixing around them silently. **(F5)** `runPiRpc` settles abnormal exits on `exit` (cumora lesson: grandchildren hold the pipes, `close` may never fire) via a grace-bounded, close-cancelable settle path. RED-first: 17 fix-specific tests fail on pre-fix code (stash `src/`); 6 controls green on both trees; suite 2566 → 2586 (3 pre-existing environment-dependent failures reproduced on clean HEAD, unrelated). Plan doc docs/requirements/040-class-aware-feedback-and-reviewer-calibration-v0.3.1.md; full-read study docs/requirements/039-reference-repos-full-read-v0.3.x.md (docs-only commit b0b6895b). `npx tsc --noEmit` strict-clean.

- **v0.3.0 — the run can no longer end at zero: partial phases + continue (harness-research redesign).** Comparative study of 4 harnesses read in full source (DeepSeek Harness, Codex-Rust, SWE-agent, cumora) plus full-text engineering literature (Anthropic x2, OpenAI harness-engineering, Cognition x2, ghuntley Ralph, alatirok, Tian Pan, Aider) — every external system treats verification as feedback into the model's context rather than harness-side verdicts, bounds loops with budgets+reminders, and structurally cannot end a run with zero preserved work. The five consecutive track-07 deaths were ALL machine-interaction kills ending PARTIAL 0/N with hours of work discarded. Changes: (1) a phase that exhausts its attempts is marked `partial`, its best attempt preserved as a labeled git stash (spec-dir and bookkeeping excluded; honors SUPER_DEV_NO_DIRTY_QUARANTINE), and the pipeline CONTINUES to the next phase — `convergenceBlocked` is never set anymore; the outer convergence loop re-enters non-green phases bounded by the global budget (SWE-agent get_best semantics). (2) budget reminders are MODEL-VISIBLE from attempt 2 (attempt number + repeating failure signatures + explicit change-strategy instruction — Codex rollout_budget model). (3) a merely-WEAK RED review (explicit weak verdict, no contradictions) proceeds to implementation with the weakness analysis as advisory context — the post-RED oracle (tests must go green) stays the deterministic endpoint; the run-10-39 Catch-22 burned MAX_RED_RETRIES tdd-guide re-authors on strength wording. Contradictions (jointly unsatisfiable) and empty/invalid verdicts keep the fail-closed re-author path. Workflow summary surfaces partial counts. RED-first: 5 flipped/new tests fail on pre-fix code. Research + design: docs/requirements/037-harness-research-and-v0.3.0-architecture.md.

### Fixed

- **v0.2.11 — judge challenge-test missing-evidence exemption** (run 2026-08-19T14-54-22-165Z, fifth consecutive death on pi-omisis track 07): the impl-no-progress judge's CORRECT 0.92-confidence `challenge-test` diagnosis (spec-01's stale SCENARIO-035 opaque pin vs spec-07's confirmed-RED closed-schema oracle — jointly unsatisfiable) was DISCARDED by evidence verification (`route "challenge-test" requires at least 1 evidence item`), no route actuated, and the run terminated at 0/12 phases. `challenge-test` joins `DIAGNOSIS_DRIVEN_MISSING_OK` — the v0.2.5 exclusion rationale ("can drop an accepted RED gate") applied equally to the already-exempt re-author-tests; challenge-test is additionally bounded by MAX_CHALLENGE_REAUTHORS and its re-authored test must still pass RED strength review. FABRICATED/MALFORMED evidence still discards on every route. New `firstCitedTestFile(diagnosis)` targets the re-author defect at the test the diagnosis names (run 14-54's stale pin) instead of the phase's own RED file when evidence is empty. RED-first: 5 new/flipped tests fail on pre-fix code (tests/judge.test.ts); plan doc docs/requirements/038-judge-challenge-test-exemption-v0.2.11.md.

### Fixed
- **Subprocess-backend spawn resilience: RPC same-session corrective retry, structured_output tool-ification, `@file` task delivery, skills parity (dogfood track 29 + pi-omisis run `2026-08-18T00-28-14-819Z`, ships as v0.2.10).** Every backend-attributable incident this week traced to the subprocess path (`src/pi-spawn.ts`) missing the two mechanisms the session backend always had: a corrective turn in the SAME session, and control delivery as a schema-declared tool. Track 29's spec writer ended 5 consecutive rounds with "Composing the specification now." narration (`control=null`, ROUND CAP 8 death) because control was text etiquette nothing structural enforced; pi-omisis Phase 2's corrective re-prompt respawned a FRESH amnesiac `pi` that re-entered the same narration loop with no memory of the 4m32s of work it had done — and the good on-disk test file was later deleted by RED cleanup because control said nothing. Four work items, grounded in live `pi --mode rpc` probes plus a full read of the pi-subagents spawn machinery: **(W1)** `spawnAgent` defaults to a new RPC execution path (`--mode rpc --no-session`, task via the first stdin `prompt` event — never argv; kill-switch `SUPER_DEV_NO_RPC_SPAWN=1` keeps the json one-shot), completion = id-matched `response` + `agent_settled` (order-independent), and the missing-control corrective is a second `prompt` event into the SAME live process — same session, same memory, no respawn (probe-verified deviation: a live `follow_up` after settle is acked but never runs); exactly one corrective turn, same wall-clock budget, abort ladder unchanged. **(W2)** the json fallback writes the task to a 0600 temp file and passes `@<path>` when task text exceeds `TASK_ARG_LIMIT=8000` — the last spawn embedded 28444 chars in argv, visible in `ps`/audit and in the EDR pre-exec argv-scan SIGKILL class. **(W3)** new `src/subprocess-structured-output.ts` runtime extension (loaded via `-e`, env `SUPER_DEV_SO_SCHEMA`/`SUPER_DEV_SO_CAPTURE`): registers a `structured_output` tool wrapping the control schema (`{value: …}`, local-`$ref` rewrite), validates in-tool with a zero-import STRUCTURAL checker (≤8 self-correctable errors; authoritative completeness stays the parent-side gate), writes the capture 0600 and returns `terminate:true` — the tool call ITSELF ends the turn, so announce-without-control has no failure surface; parent pre-unlinks stale captures, re-validates parent-side, and merges `control = toolCapture ?? <control>-text` (text contract retained as fallback) with the spawn log recording `structured_output: captured|text-fallback|absent`. **(W4)** `--no-skills` dropped by default so every subprocess agent can load skills (session-backend parity, per user directive); `SUPER_DEV_NO_SKILLS=1` restores isolation. `resolvePiBinary` also gained package-root validation (under vitest the old ladder spawned `node vitest.mjs` → exit 1). New suites `tests/rpc-driver.test.ts` (13) + `tests/subprocess-structured-output.test.ts` (13) + `tests/pi-spawn-v0210.test.ts` (15 unit + 2 real-pi E2E behind `SUPER_DEV_SPAWN_E2E=1`: full RPC+structured_output round trip ~15s, and same-session corrective recovering `control=null` → `structured_output {summary:"recovered"}` ~16s — the track-29/pi-omisis death class, fixed); argv pins updated in `tests/pi-spawn.test.ts` + `tests/pi-spawn-control-retry.test.ts` (pinned to the json fallback they always tested). RED-first verified (stash `src/`, keep untracked new tests: 13 fix-specific v0210 tests failed on pre-fix code while 55 controls across the three suites stayed green on both trees; suite-level collection failures add a few more depending on runner accounting); dual-review remediation landed (code-reviewer CHANGES REQUESTED F-1..F-9: capture-file reset before every corrective attempt so a partial capture cannot mask text-channel recovery — the F-1 high, pinned RED-first both paths; rpc-path `structured_output` channel audit log; structural-validation wording + schema parity pin; hermetic `tests/pi-spawn-rpc-run.test.ts` runPiRpc suite (5) covering corrective-once/budget-skip/error-skip/abort-ladder/old-pi-quick-exit+hint; sentinel-based instruction idempotency for role prompts that mention the tool name; spawn-error driver dispose; unused-dep cleanup); plan doc at `docs/requirements/035-subprocess-spawn-resilience-v0.2.10.md` with probe-verified deviations recorded; `npx tsc --noEmit` strict-clean.
- **G6 — baseline verification now runs in the failing subject's language + module, not the run's primary (run `2026-08-19T08-32-47-962Z`, ships as v0.2.9).** A frontend-track run's whole-repo gate surfaced a pre-existing failure in the out-of-scope **Go** `snow` package; the env-blocker baseline check then verified it with the run's PRIMARY language (`pnpm run test`, which passes at baseline) instead of Go — mis-tagging a pre-existing failure as a NEW regression, which defeated the env-blocker machinery and stalled the phase. `resolveInScopePassWithBaseline` now GROUPS the out-of-scope failure blocks by each block's OWN detected language (`detectFailureBlockLanguage`: go/rust/python/node) and verifies each group with the matching runner: Go/Rust/Python OVERRIDE the run's primary language, node-family blocks keep it. For Go it resolves the owning module via `resolveGoModuleForPackages` (longest `module` prefix match across nested `go.mod` files) and runs the baseline `go test ./<pkg>` inside that module dir (`baseline.ts` gains a `moduleSubdir` that relocates the merge-base command into the nested module). A regression in ANY language group strips the lenient pass; all-preexisting/unknown keeps it. General across go/rust/python/node; unknown-language blocks fall back to today's behavior (safe direction — never a false regression). New `tests/baseline-language-parity.test.ts` (8: language detection incl. the jest-`FAIL <file>`-vs-Go-package trap, Go package + Python file parsers, nested-module resolution, and the run-08-32 integration: a Go failure on a node-primary track verifies with Go+module and a Go regression strips the pass / a pre-existing Go failure keeps it); existing baseline-verify suite stays green.
- **G5 — richer per-iteration run-log stream.** The run log now streams, each try/attempt: what **tdd-guide** did (test files + its summary), what the **implementer** did (created/modified/deleted files, tests-pass count, summary), alongside the existing RED-review reject reason — so an operator can see *why* a review rejected and *what* each agent produced without opening artifacts. Pure logging (no worktree writes) — zero interaction with phase gating. Version 0.2.8→0.2.9; `npx tsc --noEmit` strict-clean.
- **No artifact may reference a non-existent thing — prevent · detect · cure · unblock (run `2026-08-19T08-32-47-962Z`, ships as v0.2.8).** Phase-1 RED looped 7 tries and could not converge because AC-01 mandated preserving an "existing direct-REST STEP connection type/schema/routes/contract" that does not exist in the codebase (zero `"STEP"` connection constants outside the new `stepmcp` stubs): the RED strength reviewer correctly kept demanding SCENARIO-001 bind that "existing" contract to concrete unchanged values while the tdd-guide correctly could not find any such contract to bind. Four layers, all oracle-guarded, judge-budget/replan-budget-bounded: **(G1 cure)** the RED no-progress judge gains `replan-upstream` — an evidence-required route that sends an upstream-artifact defect (an AC referencing a non-existent baseline; a spec citing a non-existent scenario/AC) back to the owning stage via the existing replan circuit (`triggerReplanForFindings`), so the run ends `replan` and auto-resumes at requirements/bdd/spec instead of re-authoring a doomed test until budget exhaustion; not-routable degrades to HITL. **(G2 prevent)** the requirements writer must ground any "preserve/extend the existing X" claim against the actual codebase (else treat as greenfield or record an open question); the bdd writer must never mint an AC absent from requirements; the spec writer must reference only AC-NN/SCENARIO-NNN that exist and ground every code reference. **(G3 detect)** the requirements-reviewer gains a BLOCKING existence-grounding dimension (D7) — an AC preserving a non-existent baseline is a blocking finding; the bdd/spec reviewers explicitly block dangling/invented references. **(G4 unblock RED)** the tdd-guide may now create NEW declaration-only scaffolding (types/interfaces/consts/enums, unimplemented function signatures) + fixtures so a compiled-language test COMPILES and still fails RED (fixing the `broken`-oracle oscillation on undefined symbols); a new boundary `scaffold` category is allowed (distinct from `production`), and when the boundary is ambiguous the judge arbitrates via `allow-scaffold` (reads the spec + files, blesses spec-justified declaration-only scaffolds). The RED oracle remains the final guard — a scaffold that implements behavior flips `green` and is rejected; editing EXISTING production stays forbidden. New `JUDGE_ROUTES`: `replan-upstream`, `allow-scaffold` (both evidence-required, not missing-evidence-exempt). New/updated tests: judge route membership + evidence-required discard/route, boundary `scaffold` + `approveScaffoldPaths`, writer/judge prompt discipline, and the RED no-progress judge route offering. (Per-phase record docs and phase-scoped test runs are tracked as follow-ups — v0.2.9.) Version 0.2.7→0.2.8; `npx tsc --noEmit` strict-clean.
- **Env-blocker v0.2.6 review polish (ships as v0.2.7):** two non-blocking defects found reviewing v0.2.6. **D1 (doc):** the in-loop comment at `src/stages/implementation.ts` claimed *"capture the phase-start dirt snapshot ONCE, before the first tdd/implementer dispatch"* but no capture happens there — the snapshot is captured ONCE at stage entry (run-start) and persisted across convergence iterations per sd26-CR-1; the comment now describes the run-start (not per-phase) provenance semantics accurately. **D2 (behavior, cosmetic):** on the judge `implementer-retry` override path, `ownDirtFeedback` was appended to the implementer retry feedback both directly and nested inside `envJudgeOverrideFeedback`, duplicating every `out-of-scope edit (this run): <path>` line; the nested copy is removed (it is always appended directly), so each undeclared-edit line appears exactly once while the override diagnosis still reaches the implementer. New RED-first test in `tests/implementation-env-blocker.test.ts` (SCENARIO-001: override path names each undeclared edit exactly once); the product fall-through regression guard and all v0.2.6 tests stay green. Suite 2488 tests (155 files; 1 pre-existing environment-dependent git-identity failure unrelated to this change); `npx tsc --noEmit` strict-clean.
- **Env-blocker provenance, re-gate re-classification, judge arbitration, bounded guidance re-entry (runs `2026-08-19T01-47-29-690Z` + `2026-08-19T05-09-21-800Z`, ships as v0.2.6): the environmental-blocker machinery misfired twice on the same shape — the implementer's own edits breaking a pre-existing out-of-scope test with no foreign dirt present — and the second misfire was self-inflicted: the quarantine stashed the implementer's live `WriterId` fix, the still-failing re-gate rode a stale `class=environment` into the judge whose route set could not express its correct "NOT environmental" diagnosis, and the user's `retry-with-guidance` decision was dead-lettered by the convergence block. G1: a run-start porcelain snapshot (one per run, persisted across convergence iterations, partitioning every phase) separates FOREIGN dirt (predates this run — the only quarantineable class) from this run's own work (any phase's undeclared edits: never stashed, named in the retry feedback); `classifyGateFault` row (2) now requires proven foreign dirt — a clean-at-start tree classifies product. G2: a still-failing post-quarantine re-gate re-classifies its own evidence and falls through to implementer retry with the re-run's errors as feedback truth (the environmental judge is reachable only while foreign dirt plausibly explains the failure). G3: `implementer-retry` joins `JUDGE_ROUTES` as a diagnosis-driven, missing-evidence-exempt route offered at the env-blocker boundary — a judge whose grounded diagnosis contradicts the classifier overrides it, audited in the log + `.environment-faults.jsonl` ledger, with the diagnosis joined to the implementer's retry prompt. G4: the FIRST `retry-with-guidance` at the env-blocker escalation grants a bounded re-entry (guidance persisted AND the convergence windup trip skipped — one-shot per phase EVER, persisted across convergence iterations via the stage control) so the decision actually reaches the next pass. Route glosses added to the judge prompt so a never-before-offered route is choosable. 22 fix-specific tests RED-first verified (17 fail on pre-fix code; pure-classification controls green on both trees — the foreign-only-stashing test is FIX-pinned per adversarial review sd26-F6); adversarial review CONTEST findings sd26-F1..F7 remediated (per-phase-EVER provenance + guidance grants persisted across convergence iterations, grant consumed only after persistence, observed post-quarantine provenance, stale-comment/dead-clause cleanup); suite 2477 → 2486.
- **Judge missing-evidence discard killed the RED no-progress recovery (run `2026-08-19T02-01-12-840Z`, ships as v0.2.5): the one non-HITL exit from the RED strength livelock was thrown away, failing the run at 0/7 phases.** In that run Phase 1's RED tests were valid (compiled, ran, failed for the intended reason, scenario coverage passed) but the RED *strength* review kept returning NOT STRONG; after the retry ceiling the diagnostic judge fired at `stage9.red-no-progress.phase-01` (routes offered `re-author-tests`, `fix-environment`), correctly diagnosed a test-quality defect, and chose `re-author-tests` — but attached ZERO evidence items, so `verifyJudgeEvidence`'s `route "re-author-tests" requires at least 1 evidence item` rule DISCARDED the sound verdict; the run fell through to terminal no-progress and HITL escalation (unavailable headless) and died at 0/7. v0.2.4's judge work fixed a different failure (timeouts) and did not touch this discard path. Root cause: the missing-evidence *degrade* was gated to `escalate-now` only (F4/RC4), so a missing-evidence verdict on the bounded re-run recovery routes still hard-discarded even though they never acquit a gate (INV-1) and are budget-bounded (INV-3). **Fix (J5):** `re-author-tests` and `fix-environment` are now treated as DIAGNOSIS-DRIVEN routes — a MISSING-evidence verdict (empty evidence list) ROUTES with a documented INV-2 exemption in `.judge.jsonl` (parity with the `continue` zero-evidence exemption) instead of discarding, so the RED loop restarts with the diagnosis. The fabrication guard is unchanged: FABRICATED evidence (a quote that fails verification) and MALFORMED evidence (all items empty/whitespace — the B4 class) still DISCARD on these routes; `challenge-test` stays non-exempt (it can drop an already-accepted RED gate); and the allowed/confidence gates still apply after the evidence gate (a below-confidence exempt verdict still escalates). New tests: six in `tests/judge.test.ts` (SCENARIO-001..006: re-author/fix-environment empty-evidence route + documented audit line; fabricated-quote discard; malformed discard; challenge-test non-exempt discard; below-confidence escalate), all RED-first verified against pre-fix code; the `escalate-now` empty-evidence degrade and every existing judge test stay green. Suite 2471 → 2477 tests (155 files; 1 pre-existing environment-dependent git-identity failure unrelated to this change); `npx tsc --noEmit` strict-clean.
- **Judge resilience: timeout budget, retry-on-timeout, and a deterministic test-suspect advisory (run `2026-08-19T03-16-50-261Z`, ships as v0.2.4).** The impl-no-progress judge — the circuit breaker designed to route `re-author-tests` for a defective RED — timed out at the hardcoded 120s wall while diagnosing a born-broken RED test (an unsatisfiable CJK `.sort()` collation literal no implementation can satisfy), the timeout consumed the per-signature budget slot so call 2/2 never happened, and the headless terminal stop never named the most likely cause. Three fixes, grounded in durable-execution and LLM-production research (Temporal retries timed-out attempts within `MaximumAttempts` and reserves `non_retryable_error_types` for deterministic failures; OpenAI/Anthropic SDKs default 600s + 2 retries with 408 explicitly retryable; LLM latency is structurally heavy-tailed; CI test-classification literature: a consistently-failing test is broken, and classifiers must stay advisory at the human boundary): **(J1)** the judge wall-clock cap becomes a lazy `judgeTimeoutMs()` honoring `SUPER_DEV_JUDGE_TIMEOUT_MS`, default 120s → 240s (still bounded, ~3.4x the observed ~71s typical judge latency, 40% of the 600s SDK industry default); **(J2)** a timeout that produced no control retries ONCE within the existing per-signature budget (Temporal-faithful attempt counting — no budget refunds; a second timeout, any deterministic infra error, or an exhausted run budget degrades as before with the attempt count audited); **(J3)** when the GREEN-loop no-progress terminal stop carries `tdd-targets-still-red` evidence (RED targets never greened while the implementer actively changed code), the stop log, escalation message, and findings gain an explicit test-suspect advisory naming the legal next actions (re-author the RED with the evidence, fix the environment, accept the limitation) — deterministic floor, advisory only, never auto-triggering a re-author. New tests: five in `tests/judge.test.ts` (J1 default+env override, J2 retry-and-route, J2 double-timeout attempt accounting, non-timeout no-retry control, run-budget control) and `tests/implementation-test-suspect.test.ts` (advisory surfaced across log/message/findings; no-still-red control), all RED-first verified against pre-fix code. Suite 2461 → 2468 tests (155 files, all green); `npx tsc --noEmit` strict-clean.
- **Fault-classified actuation, normalized failure signatures, reused-worktree quarantine, and an environment-fault ledger (run `2026-08-18T01-02-50-093Z`, ships as v0.2.3): Stage 9 is no longer an open-loop retry controller driving one actuator against every fault class.** The motivating run burned 4h39m and was cancelled at phase 1/4 after **11 identical implementer retries** re-proved the same pre-existing out-of-scope `internal/services/snow` failure — the gate's correct "regression at clean merge-base" verdict had no actuator, volatile output noise hashed the identical failures into 11 distinct no-progress signatures, and the shared worktree carried prior runs' dirt. Four principle-derived fixes, all grounded in a new shared `src/fault-classification.ts` (pure TypeScript, no LLM in the classification floor): **(PRA) classification before actuation** — on any build-gate failure a deterministic classifier runs BEFORE actuator selection; when every failure is out-of-scope, `baselineCheck=regression`, and own-scope evidence is green, the fault is an *environmental-blocker* and the implementer is NEVER re-spawned for it: foreign dirt (git actual outside the claimed set ∪ declared scope ∪ harness bookkeeping) is quarantined with a scoped `git stash push -u`, the gate re-runs EXACTLY ONCE (baseline memo cleared so a stale `regression` verdict cannot poison the re-run), and a still-blocked phase routes to the judge (`fix-environment`) at FIRST occurrence carrying both evidence packets; genuine in-scope/mixed failures keep today's retry semantics, and the synthetic `[baseline-verify]` block is hoisted into the exported `BASELINE_VERIFY_ERROR_PREFIX` (gate and classifier read ONE literal) and excluded from the failure tally. **(PRB) signature noise normalization** — `normalizeSignatureText` strips volatile noise (ISO-8601 timestamps, UUIDs, durations, `(cached)`/`[cached]` markers) BEFORE the whitespace-collapse/800-char cap, so >800 chars of leading noise can no longer displace discriminating content and the run's 11 noise-differing replicas normalize to ONE signature — `repeatedNoProgress` now trips at attempt 2 through the existing judge/HITL machinery, while failing-package/error-class differences still compare unequal (no over-normalization). **(PRC) reused-worktree hygiene** — `runSetup` on a reused or resumed track detects foreign uncommitted state (same canonical exclusions) and quarantines it recoverably, logging the paths, stash ref, and `git stash pop`; fresh tracks and the user's main checkout are never touched. **(PRD) environment-fault ledger** — every quarantine and judge-environmental verdict appends one JSON line (`{kind, paths, stashRef, reason}`) to `<specDir>/.environment-faults.jsonl`, and setup surfaces the track's prior-fault count. ONE kill switch governs every new automatic worktree mutation: `SUPER_DEV_NO_DIRTY_QUARANTINE=1` disables both quarantines with a detection-warning only. Safety: stash-based only (never `checkout`/`reset`/`clean`/`drop`), and any mechanism failure (git error, unwritable ledger) degrades to a warning plus judge routing / plain proceed — never fatal. New suites: `tests/fault-classification.test.ts` (47), `tests/signature-noise.test.ts` (9), `tests/implementation-env-blocker.test.ts` (25), `tests/setup-dirty-quarantine.test.ts` (14), plus the `BASELINE_VERIFY_ERROR_PREFIX` hoist pin in `tests/baseline-verify.test.ts` — suite 2362 → 2461 tests (154 files, all green); `npx tsc --noEmit` strict-clean. Dual code+adversarial review remediations pinned: exit-0 pathspec-miss stashes can't count as success (phantom-success guard), the env-blocker terminal stop sets `convergenceBlocked`, routed guidance persists to track user-notes, and a post-quarantine re-run with a failing fresh deliverable check re-classifies as product, never environmental.
- **Implementation-stage harness defects RC8–RC12 (macOS STEP E2E runs 10-39/15-07, ships as v0.2.2): the blocker has moved inside Stage 9, and it was the harness, not the content.** Both runs converged requirements→BDD→design→spec cleanly, then died in implementation on four harness defects, each verified against the run logs and fixed with RED-first tests. **(RC8) RED-review rejections were reported as "tests passed before implementation"** — the review-weak verdict force-set `green-weak-test` and the failure-reason template discarded the true reason, so logs, judge context, HITL escalation, and the convergence ledger all lied while the oracle said `red` every round; review rejections now carry their own `review-weak` status and `red-review-rejected: <true reason>` evidence, and green-weak prefers the recorded reason (hollow-test) over the fixed template. **(RC9) deliverable `contains:` on code files matched comment-stripped text while the TDD prompt explicitly allowed "a comment"** — Go test names cannot contain `-`, so SCENARIO tags were comments and `missing pattern SCENARIO-00[1-9]` fired 3× on a file containing SCENARIO-003..009; the gate now emits an honest, actionable error naming comment-stripping when the raw text matches, and the prompt contract states comments don't count. **(RC10) Go greenfield detection missed cross-module/same-package shape** — behavior tests referencing not-yet-declared symbols in an EXISTING package classified `broken` forever (run 10-39's 6-retry death); shape 3 blesses greenfield RED when every `undefined:` ref is imported-from-inside-the-module (alias-guarded — external-package symbols stay `broken`) and declared nowhere at top level, with a levenshtein≤2 typo guard (a typo of an existing symbol stays `broken`). **(RC11) task-contract precedence** — spec tasks stating declaration-level observables ("must declare type X") collided with the RED-review standard demanding behavior tests, an unsatisfiable oscillation; both the RED-review and TDD prompts now bind the test level to the task's stated observable. **(RC12) worktree hygiene** — a fresh worktree had no `node_modules`, so the whole-monorepo build failed on unrelated packages and the implementer "fixed" unrelated files (auth-service type shims, snow, report files persisting across runs); setup now best-effort bootstraps dependencies (frozen lockfile, pm-detected, `SUPER_DEV_NO_BOOTSTRAP` kill-switch, `SUPER_DEV_BOOTSTRAP_TIMEOUT_MS`, yarn-classic vs Berry flags, 64MB maxBuffer) and GREEN-phase out-of-scope edits are recorded as low non-blocking findings with a spec-contract scope basis (implementer self-claims are NOT scope; RED test files excluded; rename/quoted porcelain paths parsed). Suite 2347 → 2362 tests (150 files, all green); `npx tsc --noEmit` strict-clean. Dual code+adversarial review remediated (porcelain literal-`\n` split bug, external-package greenfield hole, yarn flag/maxBuffer gaps, self-claim-defeatable scope basis — all fixed before commit).
- **Whole-codebase review remediation (spec-28, ships as v0.2.1 under the patch-99 → minor rollover): all 7 HIGH blockers and all 23 MEDIUM contracts from a 10-agent dual review of `src/` are fixed, each with its own regression test.** Provenance: ten parallel reviewers (five code + five adversarial) swept the full tree at v0.1.99 (commit 441b97df) and returned 75 raw findings, deduplicated into 7 HIGH / 23 MEDIUM / 29 dispositioned LOW items and executed as 35 acceptance criteria across 7 phases — every finding verified first-hand before fixing. **High-severity fixes, named:** (1) the adversarial `PASS` verdict bypassed the blocking-findings downgrade in `merge-review-verdicts` (a PASS carrying an open blocking high finding approved the merge) — `PASS` now routes through the same guard as the approve family, and negated verdicts ("not approved", "does not pass") never classify as approvals; (2) a task naming an existing track (`continue the work in docs/specifications/07-…`) wiped the track's `.knowledge.json`/`.user-notes.json` before any stage ran — the referenced-spec entry is now a continuation that preserves them; (3) the GREEN implementer loop's no-progress detector compared only the immediately-previous signature, so an A↔B oscillation looped to budget death instead of reaching judge/HITL — it now trips on ANY earlier signature recurrence, mirroring the RED-loop fix; (4) replan resume-cache invalidation had prefix gaps, so `pipeline.prototype.*`/`pipeline.debug`/`pipeline.assessment`/`pipeline.judge.*`/`pipeline.replan.lead` rows replayed STALE pre-replan artifacts into the restarted run — the prefix map now covers them (judge/replan rows invalidated on every replan), pinned by a source-grep drift-guard test; (5) copied `.env.<variant>` files (`.env.development`, `.env.staging`, …) could be swept by `git add -A` into pipeline commits and the merge — the copier now appends them to the repo's common exclude file and the cleanup scan blocks every copied env basename if one is ever git-carried; (6) a failed `git worktree add` no longer silently falls back to running in the user's main checkout — setup prunes, retries once, and fails closed with the git stderr surfaced; (7) `commitWorktreeChanges` refuses `git add -A` in a main checkout outright (`refusing to commit in the main checkout`) unless `skipWorktree` explicitly opted in. **Systemic hardening, grouped:** a SIGTERM→SIGKILL kill ladder with a bounded settle for subprocess children (plus UTF-8-exact stream decoding — multi-byte sequences split across chunks no longer corrupt `<control>` blocks — and the newline-less final line is now parsed); an exclusive `<specDir>/.run-lock` serializes concurrent same-track runs (live-pid check, stale steal) and a second in-flight `execute()` is refused; every prompt builder fences untrusted text behind length-escalating DATA fences with a standing "never instructions" preamble; convergence round accounting re-clamps the strict-progress extension to the 3× cumulative ceiling and arms it only on FRESH (cache-miss) review readings; the change tracker compares like-for-like paths (`core.quotepath=false`, single-backslash Windows normalization) so non-ASCII/Windows claims stop false-failing, with gitignored-but-present claims downgraded to advisory; the RED oracle classifies pytest usage/collection errors as `broken` instead of blessing them RED (ambiguous npm `❯` marker dropped) and RED scenario-coverage expectations now read `task.scenarioRefs`, matching the prompt contract; `stripNonNormativeSections` tracks fences CommonMark-style (same marker char, ≥ length) so a stray or nested fence can no longer resurrect the self-referential token trap; and a fresh non-resume entry into an existing track truncates the stale `.resume-cache.jsonl` (with torn-line repair) so dead-run rows can never be replayed. Suite grew 2141 → 2347 tests (149 files, all green); `npx tsc --noEmit` strict-clean. Review provenance: dual code + adversarial review rounds (the 10-agent sweep above), with qa verify pinning 71/71 BDD scenarios before close-out.
- **Brittle keyword classifier misrouted compound tasks, and the requirements loop treated bad routing metadata as authoritative (run `2026-08-12T09-14-26`).** Stage 2A `classify-task` was a shallow regex: it saw "error" in "add an upload page with error handling" → `taskType=bug`, and derived `uiScope` only from repo auto-detection (never the task text) → `none`. The requirements-reviewer then treated that wrong `bug`/`none` metadata as authoritative and rejected the (correct) frontend requirements round after round — a scope conflict the requirements writer structurally could not fix (it cannot change `taskType`/`uiScope`). Fixes: (1) **LLM classifier** — Stage 2A now runs a `task-classifier` agent (intent-aware, returns `{taskType, uiScope, rationale}` via the closed `ClassificationData` schema) instead of the regex; it degrades to the deterministic `classify-task` helper on budget-exhaustion, empty/invalid control, OR any ordinary thrown exception (the classifier is a routing convenience, never a hard dependency — Stage 2A always yields a classification), while a source-read-only boundary violation or any `FatalAbort` is RETHROWN so a safety failure never gets swallowed by the fallback. Grounded in 2026 agent-routing research: keyword/regex routers are brittle on compound intent; production systems fall through to an LLM classifier. (2) **Reviewer contract** — `requirements-reviewer.md` now treats the `Task Type`/`UI Scope` context line as a HINT, not authority; a genuine artifact-vs-classification mismatch is recorded ONCE as an `ownerStage: classify` finding (not re-opened as `requirements`). (3) **Immediate HITL escalation** — `artifactConvergenceNode` now escalates to the user on the FIRST round when a blocking finding is owned by a STRICTLY UPSTREAM stage (which the current writer cannot fix), instead of waiting for a stall repeat. New tests: `tests/classify-stage.test.ts` (LLM-result-wins, empty→fallback, budget→fallback, ordinary-throw→fallback, boundary-violation→rethrow) and an upstream-owned→immediate-escalation case in `src/stages/artifact-convergence.test.ts`.
- **Spec/review looped 8+ rounds because the plan template dropped the test-deliverables contract (run `2026-08-12T07-24-15`).** The spec-reviewer kept opening the SAME unfixable high blocking finding — "scenario-mapped phases lack formal test deliverables (`deliverables.requireScenarios`/`requireTests`)" — round after round. Root cause (verified by diffing the control JSON against the rendered doc): the spec-writer DID emit `phases[].deliverables.requireScenarios` (`.knowledge.json` carried `deliverables` ×40, `requireScenarios` ×23), but `implementation-plan.md.njk` only rendered `Scenario refs:` and never rendered the `deliverables` object. The reviewer reads the RENDERED `08-implementation-plan.md`, saw only prose scenario refs, and re-flagged the missing contract every round — a defect the spec-writer could not fix because its data was already correct; the loss was in the render template. Fixed the template to render each phase's `deliverables.requireScenarios`/`requireTests`/`requireFiles`/`requireContains`/`requireNotContains` under a "Test deliverables (enforced contract)" heading, so the enforceable metadata the reviewer checks for is present in the artifact handed to it. A COMPOUNDING second defect: `buildSpecReviewPrompt` pointed the reviewer at `specControl.planPath`/`tasksPath`, but `renderAndWrite` sets the rendered spec docs under `implementationPlanPath`/`taskListPath` (slug→camelCase+`Path`) — so the review prompt showed `Plan: N/A` / `Tasks: N/A` and never even named the plan doc that carries the deliverables. Fixed the prompt to fall back to `implementationPlanPath`/`taskListPath`. `tests/doc-path-idempotency.test.ts` gains a regression asserting the rendered plan contains `deliverables.requireScenarios` + the exact SCENARIO-NNN tags (not just prose `Scenario refs:`); `tests/prompts.test.ts` asserts the spec-review prompt carries the real plan/task paths (not N/A).
- **Multi-doc spec stage assigned the SAME index to all three docs.** On a fresh run the spec stage wrote `08-specification.md`, `08-implementation-plan.md`, AND `08-task-list.md` — three files colliding on `08`. Cause: `reserveStageDocs` resolved each slug independently via `specDoc()`, and `nextDocNumber` only excludes the SAME slug, so with none of the three yet on disk every slug computed the identical "next free" index. Added `specDocs(setup, slugs[])` which resolves the whole group together: an existing per-slug `NN-<slug>.md` is reused (idempotent across retries), while not-yet-existing slugs take DISTINCT consecutive indices from a base that excludes the group's own slugs — so the spec stage now writes e.g. `08-specification.md` / `09-implementation-plan.md` / `10-task-list.md`. `reserveStageDocs` now calls it; the orphaned unused `specDocRange` was removed. `tests/doc-path-idempotency.test.ts` gains a regression asserting the three docs get distinct consecutive indices (and still one-file-per-slug across re-runs).
- **Writer response matrix could self-verify a blocker (round-3 review).** `markConvergenceFindingsAddressedFromResponses()` honored a `status: "verified"`/`"deferred"` response regardless of who sent it, so a WRITER `reviewResponses` entry claiming `{findingId, status: "verified"}` cleared the finding out of `blockingConvergenceFindings()` — letting the writer self-clear a blocker it merely asserted fixed, contradicting the "writer responses are claims; stay blocking until reviewer verification" contract. The helper now takes a `source: "writer" | "reviewer"` mode (default `"writer"`): a writer's verified/deferred claim is CLAMPED to `addressed` (still blocking), while only a reviewer's `priorFindingResolutions` (`source: "reviewer"`) may verify/defer and clear it. Applied at the call sites: upstream + spec writer `reviewResponses` are `"writer"`; the upstream reviewer's `priorFindingResolutions` is `"reviewer"`. `tests/convergence-ledger-review-findings.test.ts` pins that a writer verified/deferred claim stays blocking and only a reviewer verify clears.
- **Upstream reviewers: three correctness bugs from post-merge review of 584ab8f.** (1) **Design failure was silently treated as an intentional skip.** `designStage` returned `null` both when design was legitimately skipped (bug fix) AND when a selected designer timed out / returned no control; `designConvergenceNode`'s `skipped: (s) => !s.design` predicate then converged `ok` on a designer failure, bypassing the new design-review gate for features that actually need design. Fixed: the skip decision is now made by CLASSIFICATION (`taskType === "bug"`), not by artifact presence; a `null` design for a non-bug task is a FAILURE that retries, and `designStage` also returns `null` (→ retry) when `renderAndWrite` fails schema/render (no `NN-design.md` written) instead of approving a phantom design. A general "writer produced no artifact" guard was also added to the convergence loop so an empty/failed writer output on any reviewed stage retries instead of slipping past the deterministic + review gates. (2) **Suggestion-only reviews were rejected.** The reviewer contract says "APPROVED WITH REVISIONS / COMMENTS" (only non-blocking suggestions) should PASS, but the loop used the strict `isApprovedVerdict()`, which rejects any verdict containing "revision" — so a review with a single low-severity suggestion spun to budget exhaustion. Added a dedicated `reviewVerdictApproves()` that approves affirmative verdicts (and is AND-ed with `!reviewHasBlockingFinding`, so a blocking finding still blocks regardless of wording); the three reviewer agents' verdict vocab was aligned to the canonical "APPROVED WITH COMMENTS = suggestion-only pass". (3) **Writer/reviewer response matrices were never applied to the ledger.** Unlike spec-convergence, upstream convergence never called `markConvergenceFindingsAddressedFromResponses()`, so a prior finding the rewrite claimed to resolve (`reviewResponses`) or the reviewer confirmed resolved (`priorFindingResolutions`) was never recorded against the ledger at all. Both matrices are now applied each round: a writer response marks the finding `addressed` (its CLAIM — still blocking, re-surfaced to the reviewer until VERIFIED, matching the spec-convergence "keep prior findings until verified" contract), and a reviewer `priorFindingResolutions` entry with `status: verified` clears it. New regression tests in `src/stages/artifact-convergence.test.ts` (now 9) cover all three: suggestion-only→converge, reviewResponses→addressed(+log), bug-fix-skip vs designer-timeout-retry vs render-failure-retry; `tests/convergence-ledger-review-findings.test.ts` pins the addressed-stays-blocking-until-verified semantics. `npx tsc --noEmit` strict-clean; full vitest suite green.

### Added
- **Upstream shift-left reviewers: requirements / BDD / design now get their own Fagan-style review→fix convergence loop, with HITL stall escalation.** Previously only the spec stage had an LLM review gate, so a defect owned by an upstream artifact (an ambiguous AC, an uncovered scenario, an undefined interface contract) was only caught at spec review — where the spec-writer cannot fix it, causing the spec/review loop to spin on upstream-owned findings. Three new read-only reviewer agents (`agents/requirements-reviewer.md`, `agents/bdd-reviewer.md`, `agents/design-reviewer.md`) now inspect each artifact against stage-specific dimensions (requirements: testability/unambiguity/consistency/completeness/feasibility/resolved-decisions; BDD: AC-coverage/path-completeness/observable-behavior/GWT-integrity/consistency; design: interface-contracts/feasibility-grounding/requirements-consistency/data-flow/complexity/numeric-constants) and return a verdict + blocking/suggestion findings — caught at the SOURCE so they never cascade into the spec. Each runs INSIDE its stage's convergence loop: `artifactConvergenceNode` gained an optional `review` step that runs after the deterministic validator passes; a non-approved verdict (or any blocking finding) records the findings to the convergence ledger and feeds them back into the next writer attempt (a reviewer-only retry can never masquerade as a fix). When the SAME blocking findings recur unchanged across rounds (a stall), the run escalates to the user via the existing HITL `escalate` callback (kind `stagnation`, bounded by `ESCALATION_RETRY_CAP`); `accept-limitation` converges, `abandon` aborts, `retry-with-guidance` re-runs with persisted guidance. The design stage becomes a `designConvergenceNode` (no deterministic gate — quality is judged only by the reviewer — and it converges immediately when design is skipped for a bug fix). Each reviewer renders its own `NN-<slug>-review.md` via the render pipeline (new `RequirementsReviewData`/`BddReviewData`/`DesignReviewData` schemas + templates + `buildUpstreamReviewPrompt`) and, like the other reviewers, can be mapped to a different model through `~/.super-dev/config.json` → `agentModels` (cross-model review). New `src/stages/artifact-convergence.test.ts` + `tests/upstream-review-integration.test.ts`: control-flow cases plus a real-render smoke/integration run that drives the requirements convergence node end-to-end through the render pipeline against a temp project dir and asserts the `NN-requirements.md` + `NN-requirements-review.md` docs are written to disk, and that a reject→fix→approve cycle re-writes the SAME files in place (no index drift). `npx tsc --noEmit` strict-clean; full vitest suite green.


- **Deterministic doc-path reservation + stage/agent stream logging.** The exact output filename for each stage is now RESOLVED and LOGGED up front — the render pipeline reserves the stage's `NN-<slug>.md` path(s) (primary + every multi-doc `additionalDocs` entry) via `reserveStageDocs()`, emits `<stage>: doc → NN-<slug>.md` into the streaming log, and renders to those exact reserved paths (matched by slug, so multi-doc order can't drift). Combined with the idempotent `specDoc` (reuse-existing-else-next-index), a stage that re-runs writes the SAME files in place instead of allocating a new index. Writer stages also stream a `<stage>: agent <name> working` line at start so it is visible which specialist is running each stage. `tests/doc-path-idempotency.test.ts` covers reservation stability, the `doc →` log, and one-file-per-slug across re-runs.
- **Cross-model review + independent RED test-quality gate + 3-level implementation display.** Three composed capabilities that make review independent of the author (per the 2026 "no output graded by its own model" trust model — Kitchen Loop arXiv:2603.25697):
  1. **Per-agent model config (cross-model review).** `~/.super-dev/config.json` → `agentModels` maps agent roles to `provider/id` models, so `code-reviewer`/`adversarial-reviewer` can run on a *different* model than the implementer. Precedence A: `call.model` > `agentModels[role]` > global `--model`/`SUPER_DEV_MODEL` > inherited session model — a cross-model policy is NOT undone by a one-off `--model`. Resolved per-agent model appears on each `agent … start … model=` log line. Pure `resolveAgentModel` (`workflow.ts`) + `tests/agent-model-resolution.test.ts` (6).
  2. **Hollow- and weak-assertion RED gates (Plan 2).** Tier 1 (deterministic): a RED test file with NO recognizable assertion (`assertionPresenceGaps`, cross-stack) is rejected before the implementer, routed back to `tdd-guide` via the existing RED retry machinery. Tier 2 (independent, cross-model via #1): a `code-reviewer` audits the RED test cases for assertion *strength* (bound to observable behavior vs tautology/stub/impl-detail) — a `weak` verdict re-prompts `tdd-guide`; `strong` proceeds to implementation. Runs every phase. `buildRedReviewPrompt` + `RedReviewData` schema; `tests/hollow-assertion-guard.test.ts` (6) + RED-review WEAK→retry→STRONG case in `implementation-red-loop.test.ts`.
  3. **3-level implementation display.** The implementation stage now renders stage → phase → step: each TDD RED / RED review / Implementation step (and retry) persists as its own nested level-3 dashboard row (`kind: "step"`, 8-space indent) with its own ok/failed glyph, excluded from the top-level stage counter. `tests/dashboard.test.ts` +1.
- **Stage 9 implementation activity banner.** The implementation stage now updates the pi-native phase/working-message banner at each durable per-phase boundary: route specialist, TDD RED generation, RED oracle, RED boundary classification, implementation, build gate, deliverable check, change check, symbol check, post-RED oracle, commit, and summary. The dashboard phase row (`↳ Phase N/M: name`) still owns start/end timing, while the banner shows the current substep with attempt/RED-try detail such as `Implementation — Phase 1/2: Auth — TDD RED (attempt 1, try 1)`. This makes long foreground Stage 9 runs explain what they are waiting on without adding fake nested phase timing rows.
- **Unified verification convergence loop.** The main pipeline now uses one Stage 10 verification state machine instead of separate review and integration loops: every attempt starts with fresh code-review + adversarial-review + deterministic build evidence, integration runs only after those pass, and any review/build/integration fix forces the next attempt to restart at review before testing again. Terminal success now requires review approval, build green, and integration passed (or explicit no API/UI surface) on the same final code state; budget/stagnation exhaustion stops before applying an unreviewed final fix. The run state records per-attempt local timestamps, durations, review/build/integration outcomes, failure signatures, code-state hashes, and fix-change evidence under `__verificationAttempts`. Integration tester side effects are guarded by the same hybrid boundary approach as RED tests: obvious test/runtime artifacts are allowed, ambiguous paths go through the LLM boundary classifier, and production-file writes stop the loop without routing the problem to the implementer. New focused tests cover review-fix-review-integration, integration-fix-review-integration, no final unreviewed fix, and tester production-write rejection.
- **Implementation symbol/hollow-file gate — kills the "silent-empty-success" failure mode.** A new `computeSymbolGate` (`src/build-runner/gates.ts`) is AND-ed into phase-green alongside buildGate/deliverableCheck/changeGate: a claimed source deliverable that EXISTS (so it passes the deliverable + change gates) but contains NO language symbols (doc-comment-only / empty shell) now FAILS the phase, with the hollow files fed into the next implementer retry. Catches the exact failure observed in run `2026-07-28T13-59-52` (Phase 3 shipped as ~1.5KB doc-comment-only shells while the summary claimed "3/3 complete", triggering a ~49min review-fix loop + a stagnation break). Language-aware symbol probes (rust/go/python/ts-js) over comment-stripped source; never throws and degrades to pass on unreadable files / unknown language / phases with no source files (never blocks infrastructure or doc/config-only phases). New: `tests/symbol-gate.test.ts` (9); stub added to the 2 explicit build-runner test mocks. `npm run typecheck` strict-clean; full vitest suite green (1527 tests).
- **HITL escalation: super-dev now ASKS the user (ctx.ui.select) when it hits an unrecoverable blocker, then continues based on their decision.** An escalate(failure) callback is threaded from extension.ts -> RunOptions -> StageContext and fired inline at gate exhaustion (nodes.ts) + verify-loop stagnation (verify.ts) BEFORE the abort. Interactive: ctx.ui.select with options (retry-with-guidance / accept-limitation / abandon) + ctx.ui.input for guidance. Retry: rollbackWorktreeTo + appendUserNotes -> stage re-runs with guidance. Headless: report + fail. Default-on in TUI/RPC. Never throws; bounded by ESCALATION_RETRY_CAP=2. typecheck clean; 1574 tests green.
- **Mid-run user context: type during a super_dev run and it's captured + persisted for every subsequent stage.** Free-text typed while the pipeline runs (in BOTH foreground and background modes — previously the background default discarded it) is now captured and appended to a durable `<specDir>/.user-notes.json` (mirrors `.knowledge.json`: cleared at pipeline start, kept on resume, never read by agents — the pipeline injects it). Each specialist's prompt gains a `## User context (added during the run)` block carrying all notes accumulated so far, so context added mid-run reaches every remaining stage. Slash-commands (`/super-dev-stop`, `/reload`, …) still pass through unchanged. Delivery is non-interrupting (picked up at the next agent boundary, like pi-subagents' file-backed steer queue). The fragile live-steer path (`session.steer` / `onSteer` / `makeSteer` / `SteerSink` / the `activeSteerForwarder` state + setter) is REMOVED — the durable file + between-agent drain is the sole delivery contract; `applyThinkingLevel` is intentionally kept. New: `src/render/user-notes.ts` + `tests/user-notes.test.ts`; rewritten `tests/workflow-user-steer.test.ts`; updated `tests/input-handler.test.ts`; removed the two live-steer test files. Independently reviewed (approved, no blockers/issues). `npm run typecheck` strict-clean; full vitest suite green (1518 tests).
- **Inherit model settings while isolating ambient agents/extensions via explicit role extensions + excludeTools (spec-17 follow-up).** Specialist `pi` agents now keep ambient discovery disabled by default in BOTH backends: subprocess uses `--no-skills --no-extensions --no-context-files --no-prompt-templates`, while the session backend uses `DefaultResourceLoader({ noExtensions: true, noSkills: true, noContextFiles: true, ... })` plus empty AGENTS.md/CLAUDE.md overrides. This keeps repo-bundled `agents/<name>.md` as the sole role definition and prevents installed packages such as `pi-subagents` from discovering `~/.pi/agent/agents` or project agents. Research/browser capabilities still work through explicit `-e` role extensions only (`pi-web-access`, `pi-mcp-adapter`, `pi-browser-cdp-extension`). Recursion remains blocked by `excludeTools: ["super_dev"]` / `--exclude-tools super_dev`, and subprocess agents now receive a generated `<control>` contract from `controlKeys` plus one corrective retry when the control block is missing or incomplete. `tests/pi-spawn.test.ts` pins the isolated argv and role-extension behavior.

### Fixed
- **Stage-doc numbering: retries now UPDATE the same `NN-<slug>.md` instead of creating a new indexed file.** Observed in run `253-supa-report` as `06-specification.md`, `09-specification.md`, `11-task-list.md` etc. — a stage that re-ran (spec/review convergence, any gate retry) allocated a fresh index every round. Cause: `specDoc` → `nextDocNumber` only EXCLUDED the same slug from its count while OTHER slugs' files inflated it, so the second spec render landed at 09 not 06. Fix: `specDoc` now REUSES an existing `NN-<slug>.md` for that slug (overwrite in place) and only allocates a new index when none exists — idempotent, one artifact per slug across retries. Also removes a latent ambiguity in `readSpecDoc` (which returned the first glob match among duplicates). New `tests/doc-path-idempotency.test.ts` (2): a re-run overwrites its file; the multi-doc spec stage keeps one file per slug (specification/implementation-plan/task-list) across re-runs even with earlier-stage docs present. `npm run typecheck` strict-clean; full vitest suite green (1759 tests).
- **RED-loop livelock: oscillation detection + escalation (RC-3) and monorepo test-command scoping (RC-1).** Root-cause of run `2026-08-11T11-08-49-411Z`, where phase-1 RED generation ran **47 retries over ~15 hours** without stopping. **RC-3 (containment, the primary bug):** the RED no-progress detector only compared the *immediately previous* signature, so an A→B→A→B oscillation (here `red-not-confirmed` ↔ `red-polluted`) never tripped it — it kept "making progress" by alternating states (a livelock, not a deadlock). Replaced with cycle detection over the full retry-history window (stop when any prior RED failure signature recurs) plus a hard `MAX_RED_RETRIES` ceiling (default 6, `SUPER_DEV_MAX_RED_RETRIES`-overridable); on trip it fires the HITL escalation path (retry-with-guidance clears the window) before stopping. **RC-1 (the deepest cause):** when a target package has no `test` script, the RED plan fell back to the root `pnpm -r run test -- <file>` — which forwards the file arg to *every* workspace, runs their whole suite, and passes, so the new test never ran in isolation and the oracle read "green ⇒ tests passed before implementation" forever. Now: prefer a DIRECT scoped runner (vitest / `node --import tsx --test`) for the owning package; detect recursion from the root `test` SCRIPT BODY (not the resolved argv); and when only a recursive fan-out exists with no scoped runner, emit NO plan so the oracle reports a no-runner blocker instead of a false green. Also: the hollow-assertion guard is now scoped to actual TEST-named files (a test-only support fixture is no longer falsely flagged). New tests: `implementation-red-loop.test.ts` (A-B-A-B oscillation stops within a few tries), `red-oracle.test.ts` (recursive-root gets no `-r … -- <file>` plan; vitest package gets a direct scoped run), `hollow-assertion-guard.test.ts` (support artifact exempt); RED-loop edge/smoke suites updated to mock the R2 `code-reviewer` gate and use assertion-bearing RED fixtures. `npm run typecheck` strict-clean; full vitest suite green.
- **Fourth-pass review: deliverable-path worktree-escape + requireScenarios cap, and shell-command bringup safety.** (1) **requireScenarios cap (High):** the exact touched/declared evidence files (tier 1) now have their own generous budget separate from the untrusted directory-walk cap, so the tagged RED test is always read even when it is the 201st+ touched file — previously `readInto` early-returned at `MAX_FILES` and never opened it. Verified with a 210-unrelated-touched-files repro (was `missing`, now found); the tier-2 walk keeps its own bounded counter. (2) **Deliverable-path escape (High):** `requireFiles`, `requireContains`, and `deliverablesAlreadyMet` no longer read/exist-check outside the worktree — a single `resolveInsideCwd()` resolver gates every deliverable filesystem access, so a model-authored `../outside.txt` can't satisfy an assertion against an external file (reproduced: external file with the pattern now correctly fails all three). (3) **Bringup command safety (Medium):** `startService` runs model-discovered service commands via `shell:true` with the full env + `.env`, bypassing the agent bash hook — it now screens `spec.cmd` with the same `checkBashCommand` denylist and refuses a dangerous command (returns a not-ready handle, no spawn) instead of executing it. New tests: `build-runner-deliverable-check.test.ts` (tier-1 direct-read + escape-guard cases), `lifecycle.test.ts` (dangerous-bringup refused). `npm run typecheck` strict-clean; full vitest suite green.
- **Third-pass review follow-ups (requireScenarios scan, RED-review schema, TDD step glyph).** (1) `requireScenarios` no longer false-negatives in a **root-only** large repo: `collectTestFileContents` now scans the immediate parent directory of each touched/evidence FILE first (not just the resolved project roots, which collapse to `cwd` when there's a single root manifest), so the 200-file cap can't be exhausted by an unrelated subtree before the module holding the tag — reproduced the reporter's 201-unrelated-files case and confirmed the fix, with a `+`-tier dedupe so a file isn't re-counted across scan tiers. (2) `RedReviewData` is now a CLOSED schema (`additionalProperties: false`), making it strict-capable for constrained sampling like the other review schemas — reduces invalid RED-review outputs and avoidable retries (runtime already fails closed, so this was defense-in-depth); added to `session-agent-constrained-sampling.test.ts`. (3) The TDD RED and Implementation step rows now show `failed` (not a misleading `✓`) when their agent errors/times out — observability parity with the R1 fail-closed behavior. New tests: `build-runner-deliverable-check.test.ts` (+1 root-only 201-file case), `implementation-phase-subtitle.test.ts` (+1 errored-TDD step glyph). `npm run typecheck` strict-clean; full vitest suite green.
- **Fail-CLOSED hardening of the RED test-integrity gates (follow-up review of the cross-model/RED-quality changes).** A second review found the new Plan 2 gates could fail *open* — the same "proceed on missing/errored agent output" class the whole effort was meant to close. Five fixes: **(R1)** an unclassifiable/absent RED status (`unknown-*` — tdd-guide errored/timed out, returned no test files, or the runner couldn't classify) for a phase that declares expected scenarios or a `requireTests`/`requireScenarios` deliverable is now treated as a broken RED and retried, instead of silently proceeding to the implementer with no confirmed tests (and skipping the assertion + review gates); `tdd.error` is surfaced. **(R2)** the Tier-2 RED review now proceeds ONLY on an explicit `verdict === "strong"` — `weak`, an invalid verdict, a missing control object, or an agent error/timeout all route back to `tdd-guide` (previously anything but literal `"weak"` was treated as pass, so a null/errored review silently approved). **(R3)** the `tdd-coverage-classifier` call now declares all four control keys (`allCovered`, `coveredScenarios`, `missingScenarios`, `summary`) it reads — previously only two were declared, so an omitted array forced a false `coverage-incomplete` retry. **(R4)** `requireScenarios` file collection scans touched/evidence module dirs BEFORE `cwd`, so the 200-file cap can't be exhausted by an unrelated root subtree before reaching the module that holds the tag (large-monorepo false `missing scenario`). **(R5)** the workflow completion log now derives from the computed run `status`, so a green implementation with a failed review/build/integration/merge or a failed stage prints `PARTIAL`, not a bare `complete`. New tests: `implementation-red-loop.test.ts` (+2: R1 unknown-red fail-closed, R2 invalid-verdict route-back), `workflow.test.ts` (+1: R5 partial-log on hard-gate fail); existing Tier-2 test updated for the strict verdict. `npm run typecheck` strict-clean; full vitest suite green (0 failures).
- **Full-codebase review pass — verified control-flow + security fixes.** Two independent code reviews (control-flow algebra; spawn/tracking/safety) surfaced findings; each was verified first-hand before fixing. **Security:** `checkProtectedWrite` now blocks worktree-escape via `..`/absolute paths (the `^`-anchored protected-dir patterns never matched an escaping path); the bash denylist now catches `rm -rf .`/`./`/`*` and env-exfiltration (`printenv|env|export|set` piped/substituted into `curl|wget|nc`); the session debug trace writes `mode:0o600` (was world-readable, holds full session incl. any secret a tool surfaced); `rollbackWorktreeTo` rejects option-like refs and passes `--end-of-options`; `summarizeSlug` now runs with the same `noExtensions/noSkills` isolation as every other session; the subprocess `lineBuf` overflow keeps the tail instead of dropping the whole buffer (was silently staling the final assistant text). **Control-flow:** `control.ts` fenced-block regex `\s`→`\s*` (a compact ` ```json{…}``` ` block silently fell through to a later prose object — verified by reproduction); `tryCatch` wraps `finally.run` so a throwing teardown can't lose the original `FatalAbort`; `runConcurrent` stops sibling workers once one branch throws (was letting orphans spawn agents / write state after abort); `loop()` gained a `tolerant` option and now reports the ACTUAL iteration count (not `times`) on early exit; escalation budget is keyed by `(kind, stage)` not `kind` alone (a second distinct fatal gate was starved of its escalation) — this last one was itself caught by a new test finding the charge/read key mismatch in the first fix. Plus cleanups: removed the duplicate `NOOP_RESULT` constant, deprecated the unused signal-less `ctx.parallel`, documented the `phases===0` status coupling. New/updated tests: `safety.test.ts` (+3), `control.test.ts` (+1), `nodes.test.ts` (+3), `escalation-budget.test.ts` (3), plus updated `escalation.test.ts`/`tracking.test.ts` for the intended argv/key changes.
- **Fix 6 — scenario-mapped phases must declare a test deliverable (closes the gate-criteria invariant at the spec layer).** A phase that maps ≥1 BDD `SCENARIO-NNN` but declares neither `deliverables.requireScenarios` nor `deliverables.requireTests` can compile green while delivering zero test coverage — the silent-empty-success hole one level above the symbol gate. New exported `phaseTestDeliverableErrors(phases, tasks)` detects it (scenarios counted from both `phase.scenarioRefs` and task rows; scenario-less wiring/config phases exempt). Deliberately surfaced as **spec-reviewer guidance** (the Testability dimension prompt now requires a test deliverable per scenario-mapped phase, `ownerStage=spec`, blocking → Changes Requested) rather than wired into the hard deterministic trace gate, so it never destabilizes existing specs that legitimately omit deliverables. New `tests/doc-validators.test.ts` (+5): flags missing deliverable, passes for requireScenarios/requireTests, exempts scenario-less phases, counts task-mapped scenarios. `npm run typecheck` strict-clean; full vitest suite green (1719 tests).
- **Convergence hardening across Stage 7 (spec review), Stage 9 (implementation), and workflow close-out — five root-cause fixes to the "deterministic gate grading stochastic output on hidden criteria" failure class.** (1) **Anti-brittle scenario grading:** new `requireScenarios` deliverable matches stable `SCENARIO-NNN` tags in test file contents (grep, no runner spawn) instead of brittle full `it()` names; the spec prompt now steers authors to prefer it over `requireTests`, and `buildTddPrompt` surfaces required scenarios so the RED author embeds the tag verbatim. (2) **HITL escalation on implementation no-progress:** the Stage 9 attempt loop now fires the `escalate` callback (parity with gate-exhaustion + verify-stagnation) before the silent phase-fail — `retry-with-guidance` resets the no-progress window and continues instead of abandoning later phases; headless/dismissed runs fall through to the pre-existing break. `missing scenario:` deliverables join `missing test:` in routing back to RED regeneration (not the forbidden implementer). (3) **Phase-independence guard:** the deterministic spec trace gate (`phaseIndependenceErrors`) now hard-fails a phase that is a cascade-fail monolith on BOTH axes (>8 scenarios AND >5 distinct files) so over-decomposed all-or-nothing phases are rejected before implementation. (4) **Honest PARTIAL completion:** the workflow completion log surfaces `PARTIAL: implementation finished N/M phase(s)` (with convergence-blocked note) instead of a bare "complete" when Stage 9 did not finish; downstream docs/merge were already gated by `hasVerifiedImplementation`. (5) **Gate-criteria-in-prompt invariant:** `buildImplementPrompt` now surfaces the gradeable `requireFiles`/`requireContains` deliverables up front on attempt 1 (previously the implementer only learned them via post-failure retry feedback), while intentionally withholding `requireTests`/`requireScenarios` (RED author's job). New tests: `tests/prompts-tdd-deliverable-names.test.ts` (+3 implementer-prompt cases), `tests/build-runner-deliverable-check.test.ts` (+3 requireScenarios cases), `tests/doc-validators.test.ts` (+5 phase-independence cases), `tests/implementation-deliverable-wiring.test.ts` (+2 no-progress escalation cases), `tests/workflow.test.ts` (+2 honest-completion-log cases). `npm run typecheck` strict-clean; full vitest suite green (1714 tests). Grounded in arXiv:2603.17973 (TDD prompting paradox — context over procedure) and agent-loop no-progress/HITL-escalation best practices.
- **Stage 9 implementation deadlock: RED author now receives the exact `requireTests`/`requireFiles` deliverable contract, and `missing test:` deliverables route back to RED regeneration instead of the forbidden implementer.** Root-cause of run `2026-08-10T10-54-20-663Z` (only 1/3 phases implemented, Phase 2 stopped as `no-progress`): the RED-phase author (`buildTddPrompt` → `tdd-guide`) was graded by the deterministic deliverable gate on `phase.deliverables.requireTests` *name strings* but was never told those names, so it named `it(...)`/`test(...)` cases freely and the gate reported `missing test: <name>` on every attempt. The only agent allowed to add tests afterwards — the implementer — is forbidden from touching RED files (`tdd-tests-modified-during-green`), so the two gates were mutually unsatisfiable and the phase failed after the no-progress cap, abandoning all later phases. Fix: (1) `buildTddPrompt` now renders a `## Required Deliverables` block listing every `requireTests` name (to author verbatim) and `requireFiles` path, so the RED phase produces the graded names up front (context-over-procedure, per arXiv:2603.17973 "TDD prompting paradox"); (2) a `missing test:` deliverable now invalidates the accepted RED and re-runs `tdd-guide` (which receives the names) rather than instructing the implementer to add tests; (3) both implementer-facing retry blocks exclude `missing test:` items so the prompt never asks for the forbidden action. New `tests/prompts-tdd-deliverable-names.test.ts` (3) locks the prompt contract; `tests/implementation-deliverable-wiring.test.ts` SCENARIO-012 updated to assert the RED-regeneration route. `npm run typecheck` strict-clean; full vitest suite green (1699 tests).
- **Stage 10 review convergence now honors review-finding taxonomy.** Review and adversarial findings now carry status/blocking/confidence/evidence/recommendation through the prompt, rendered markdown, convergence ledger, verdict merge, and local stagnation detector. Verified/addressed/deferred findings and explicitly `blocking:false` findings no longer trigger another blind fix or a false recurring-blocker stop, even when they reuse an earlier high-severity finding id to confirm the prior issue is fixed. `Changes Requested` is downgraded to `Approved with Comments` when every structured finding is non-blocking, while open high/critical findings, explicit blockers, `needs-human`, `REJECT`, and `Blocked` remain merge-blocking.
- **Stage 10 verification no longer false-stops on count-only finding growth, and docs cannot mutate source after a blocked review.** Verification convergence now records merged review findings into the convergence ledger and uses per-blocker fingerprints for stagnation: a run like `8 findings -> 9 different findings` continues, while an actually recurring blocker after targeted fixes stops before another blind fix. Fixer prompts now preserve review finding id, file, line, detail, evidence, recommendation, and owner stage instead of reducing them to severity/title, so Stage 10c receives actionable repair context. Stage 12 documentation now runs with `source-read-only` access, its prompt/agent contract prohibits production-source and project-level doc edits, and the main pipeline only runs docs, pre-merge build, cleanup, and merge after Stage 10 has positively verified implementation with review approval, build pass, and integration pass/not-applicable proof.
- **Stage 9 implementation convergence is no longer hard-capped at five local attempts.** Implementation and RED generation now run while the global run budget allows, but stop when the same actionable failure repeats with the same change footprint. TUI activity labels now show absolute `attempt N` / `try N` instead of `attempt N/5` / `try N/5`, RED retries log `retry N` without a denominator, and terminal run-log lines explain whether Stage 9 stopped for repeated no-progress or budget exhaustion. The outer Stage 9 convergence node also no longer uses `SUPER_DEV_MAX_CONVERGE_ITERS`; it re-enters only until all phases are green, the budget is exhausted, or Stage 9 reports a no-progress block. New regressions prove repeated identical failures stop after feedback, while changing deliverable evidence can continue past the old fifth attempt and succeed on attempt 6.
- **Stage 10 and prototype convergence no longer expose hard local retry counts.** Verification convergence, the legacy integration loop, and Stage 6B prototype now use the same pattern as Stage 9: global agent budget plus repeated no-progress/stagnation detection. Stage 10 run-log lines now show `attempt N` and `integration retry N` without `/5`, budget exhaustion replaces max-attempt terminal findings, and prototype rounds log `round N` while stopping on repeated identical prototype evidence. Low-level `retry()`/`gate()` primitives and transient API retry backoff remain explicitly bounded when a caller opts into them.
- **Budget-bounded artifact/spec ambiguity convergence.** Requirements, BDD, research, and spec/review now run as clarity-convergence loops bounded by the global run budget/cancellation/non-retryable environment failures instead of local `5`-attempt caps. Requirements now fail unresolved `openQuestions`, BDD keeps retrying until every AC is covered, research treats prior `openIssues` as the next search agenda, and spec/review feedback no longer says `attempt N/5`. New regressions prove requirements, BDD, research, and spec can all pass on a sixth convergence round.
- **Package/runtime version alignment.** The runtime extension version now uses npm-valid semver (`0.1.21`) and `package.json` / `package-lock.json` track the exact same value. The version test pins all three metadata locations so `npm test` no longer prints a stale package version such as `pi-super-dev@0.3.0`.
- **Source-read-only enforcement for analysis/review/test agents.** Analysis/report stages, design/prototype, spec/spec-review, implementation classifiers/summary, code/adversarial review, and integration observer agents now declare `accessMode: "source-read-only"`. The session backend uses a read/diagnostic tool allowlist plus `edit`/`write` exclusion, the subprocess backend excludes the same tools, and the workflow runner takes a git-status snapshot around each source-read-only child call. Mutations under the active spec artifact directory remain allowed for report artifacts, but source/config changes outside that directory fail the agent call; clean tracked files and new untracked files are restored automatically, while pre-existing dirty files are reported for manual handling to avoid overwriting user work. This addresses the run where Stage 4 Debug Analysis edited `auth-service/src/*` before Stage 9.
- **Convergence ledger and owner-routed retries.** Stage loops now preserve structured convergence findings instead of replacing the last retry error. A new shared ledger records finding id, owning stage, status, blocking flag, evidence, invalidated downstream stages, and local timestamps. Spec review findings must classify `ownerStage` and prior finding resolution, spec rewrites include `reviewResponses`, and the workflow runner injects open ledger findings into later retry prompts unless a stage already supplied them. Environment failures such as `spawn pi ENOENT` are classified as non-retryable and stop fatal gates after one attempt with PATH/cwd diagnostics instead of burning all retries. Stage 9 and verification terminal blockers also write ledger findings, and the spec trace gate now catches a concrete Next.js catch-all-vs-specific-route grounding error before implementation. Regression coverage includes spec finding carry-forward, upstream owner routing, non-retryable gate stop, shared runner injection, route grounding, and a `/tmp` smoke that carries a spec-review finding into a Stage 9 TDD implementation of a tiny `multiply` feature.
- **Shared retry feedback now covers every stage-level retry surface.** Stage 9's remaining local RED/implementation retry prompts (weak-green RED, broken RED, polluted RED, prior convergence failures, build/test gate failures, deliverables, change-gate misses, and hollow-file symbol failures) now render through the shared `RetryFeedback` block while preserving the existing high-signal headings. Prototype retry rounds, subprocess corrective control-output retries, and in-process session `structured_output` corrective re-prompts also use the same location/observed/expected/missing/next-action contract. A new `retry-feedback-coverage` regression test enumerates the retrying stages/surfaces: document gates, prototype, Stage 9, Stage 10/legacy integration fix loops, and both agent backends.
- **Stage 9/10 retry loops now pass structured gate feedback instead of blind resampling.** Stage 9 now runs a read-only `tdd-coverage-classifier` after a confirmed RED oracle and RED boundary check when the spec/BDD exposes scenario refs. A failing-but-incomplete RED suite is recorded as `coverage-incomplete`, logs the missing `SCENARIO-NNN` IDs, writes that status to `implementation-evidence.jsonl`, and retries `tdd-guide` with a compact missing-scenario repair block before any implementer can run. Stage 10 review/build/integration fixer prompts now include a shared structured retry-feedback block with the previous fix's repository-change evidence plus the fresh gate failures, so allowed retries target the current rejection instead of repeating the same fix shape. New regression coverage pins scenario-coverage retries, coverage exhaustion blocking, no-baseline fallback, and verification fixer feedback.
- **Stage 9 retries now reuse accepted RED tests and repair the failed GREEN boundary.** Once a phase has a valid RED oracle, build/deliverable/symbol/post-RED failures go straight back to the implementer with the concrete failure evidence instead of starting a new `tdd-guide` run. The RED cache is invalidated only when the implementer edits confirmed RED test files, at which point TDD is run again. The TDD prompt now carries the BDD doc path, spec scenarioRefs, phase tasks, and an explicit distinction between valid missing-implementation RED failures and invalid missing-scenario-coverage RED samples; `tdd-guide` knowledge injection now includes BDD scenarios and spec tasks. Prototype retry rounds also carry the previous verdict/measurements/adjustments so that loop is feedback-driven instead of blind.
- **Stage 9 activity banner stays visible under live-stream compaction.** The latest `▶ Implementation — Phase N/M: ... — <activity>` banner is now treated as the current implementation activity anchor, so a long-running TDD/implementation/build agent cannot push the substep indicator out of the running stage section. Older activity banners still compact normally; the full run log remains complete.
- **Stage 9 RED generation now gets real runner diagnostics instead of blind `broken` retries.** The RED oracle now emits per-command diagnostics (`cwd`, argv, classified status, exit code/signal, spawn error, bounded stdout/stderr tail) through an additive `onResult` callback. Stage 9 logs those diagnostics, persists them in `implementation-evidence.jsonl`, includes the first diagnostic in RED failure reasons, and injects the runner tail into the next `tdd-guide` prompt when RED is `broken` or weak-green. This fixes the observed loop where five TDD retries only saw “tests did not compile/collect” while the actionable TypeScript/node:test error lived only in pi temp bash logs. RED-generation exhaustion is now logged truthfully as “stopped before implementation” instead of pretending the implementer failed five times.
